# Tool inventory — the stage-1 compatibility table

**Status: complete. All five groups filled (362 rows), and the traversal bound measured on a
real system — see "The traversal bound". Two decisions are deliberately left open and marked
as such: `response_format` on the service-binding tools, and whether the eighteen `low` tools
that declare no session parameters should.**

Groups filled: **system and search (34)**, **readonly (34)**,
**compact (22)**, **high (156)**, **low (116)**.

This is the table the design
(`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-08-result-error-strategies-design.md`) makes a
precondition for stage 3: *"No tool loses an input or an output field that is not in
it."* Every later migration plan is written from these rows.

The first five columns — tool, group, implementation files, visible in, current inputs —
are carried **verbatim** from `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-09-tool-inventory-rows.md`
(Task 1a, commit `d74a797`), which produced them from two committed scripts and had them
re-derived independently by two reviewers. They are not re-derived here. Where a row of
that document looks wrong, it is recorded under **Findings about the skeleton** below
rather than silently corrected.

The remaining eight columns come from reading each handler body — not its
`TOOL_DEFINITION` — on branch `feat/answer-adapter`, which is `main` plus the two Task 1a
commits. No handler was modified by this task.

## How to read the eight new columns

| column | what is in it |
|---|---|
| current default output | the object the caller receives today, field by field. Where the handler builds a JSON string and hands it to `return_response`, the fields listed are the fields **inside** that string; the MCP envelope is mentioned only where it deviates from `{ isError, content: [{ type: 'text', text }] }` |
| transformation today | how the ADT answer becomes that output — the parser by name, or "returns the document" where the body is passed through untouched |
| error decision today | what the handler treats as a failure: an HTTP status, a thrown error, a field in the document — and what it treats as a **success** that a reader might not expect |
| guarantees | every check, rejection or resolution the handler performs beyond passing the answer through, quoted from its own comment where it has one. A guarantee that depends on a field is a reason that field stays |
| new inputs | the inputs after `detail` is added and any removed parameter is gone. **`+detail`** is shorthand for "every current input unchanged, plus `detail`: `'terse' \| 'full' \| 'raw'`, default `'terse'`" |
| new terse projection | the fields `terse` carries. `full` carries the whole parsed structure and `raw` the document, per the design, so only `terse` needs stating |
| dropped | every field in *current default output* absent from *new terse projection* |
| justification | why each dropped field may go |

**The rule for the last two columns**, from the design: *a field the caller needs to make
the next call — an object name it did not already have, a transport number it must quote —
stays in `terse`; a field that only describes what just happened moves to `full`.*

Two consequences of that rule recur often enough to state once rather than in 362 rows:

- **`success: true` is dropped everywhere it appears.** It is the same claim the MCP
  envelope's `isError: false` already makes, and under the new adapter a value is
  reachable only through `ok` — so a payload asserting its own success is not merely
  redundant, it is the shape the three masking fixes removed.
- **A field that echoes the caller's own argument is dropped.** `class_name` in the answer
  to `GetClass(class_name: 'ZCL_X')` is not a name the caller "did not already have".
  Where an argument is *normalised* before use — uppercased, trimmed, resolved against a
  second object — the normalised value **stays**, because that one the caller did not have.

Where a row says *"returns the document"* in *transformation today*, the current default
output already is the raw body. Moving that tool to a `terse` projection is a **reduction
of the default**, and the row says so: a caller that parses the XML itself must ask for
`detail: 'raw'`.

## Masking: refusals reported as empty successes

This repository has fixed three defects of one shape — read masking, deletion masking,
activation masking — each a refusal delivered as `{ success: true }` with nothing in it.
The **error decision today** column names every further instance found, and this table
collects **all of them, for all five groups**. It is the list a migration plan reads to know
which rows are a projection change and which are a behaviour fix.

From **system and search**:

| tool | the masking |
|---|---|
| `GetTransaction` | unconditional. The handler is a stub: it ignores `transaction_name`, makes no request, and answers `isError: false` with `{ message: 'Not implemented' }`. Its parser `_parseTransactionXml` is dead code |
| `GetSqlQuery` | `parseSqlQueryXml` catches its own parse failure and returns `{ columns: [], rows: [], error: 'Failed to parse XML response' }`; the handler returns that with `isError: false`. A document it could not read is delivered as an empty result set |
| `GetObjectNodeFromCache` | when expanding `OBJECT_URI` throws, the failure is written **into the returned node** as `object_uri_response: 'ERROR: <message>'` and the node is answered as a success |
| `SearchObject` | a 200 whose body contains no `<adtcore:objectReference ` is answered `{ isError: false, content: [] }` — an empty `content` array with no text at all. "Found nothing" is a legitimate search answer, but the same branch swallows an unparseable or unauthorised 200, and the caller cannot tell them apart |
| `GetObjectsByType` | a 200 the regex could not read yields the text `No objects found for node_id …` with `isError: false` — same conflation as `SearchObject`, in prose rather than an empty array |
| `GetAdtTypes` | a 200 that is not a `nameditem:namedItemList` yields the text `[]` with `isError: false` |
| `DescribeByList` | an object that could not be described is **dropped from the answer** by an empty `catch {}` with no marker, so a short list reads as "these are the objects". Separately, its outer catch answers `{ isError: true, content: [] }` — an error with no message |
| `GetObjectInfo` | enrichment failure is swallowed by `catch (_e) {}`, leaving `OBJECT_DESCRIPTION` and `OBJECT_PACKAGE` `undefined` on every node. Since the enrichment call is itself broken (see Findings), this is the *only* outcome |
| `RuntimeRunClass`, `RuntimeRunProgram` | `output` is `response.data` when it is a string **and the empty string when it is not** — a body the handler did not recognise becomes `output: ''` alongside `success: true` |

From **readonly**:

| tool | the masking |
|---|---|
| the seventeen `Read*` handlers | `ReadBehaviorDefinition`, `ReadBehaviorImplementation`, `ReadClass`, `ReadDataElement`, `ReadDdl`, `ReadDomain`, `ReadFunctionGroup`, `ReadFunctionInclude`, **`ReadFunctionModule`**, `ReadInterface`, `ReadMetadataExtension`, `ReadPackage`, `ReadServiceBinding`, `ReadServiceDefinition`, `ReadStructure`, `ReadTable` — and `ReadMessageClass` / `ReadMessageClassMessage` in their own shape. A read that produced no `data` is delivered as `success: true` with `source_code: null`. `ReadProgram` is the only member that refuses instead |
| `GetEnhancementImpl` | a "not found" is answered `isError: false` with `status: 'not_found'` and the spot's metadata. It names the condition in a field, so a caller *can* branch — but it is still a refusal delivered as a success |
| `GetEnhancementSpot` | a 200 whose regexes matched nothing yields `metadata: {}` with `isError: false` |
| `GetEnhancements` | a per-include failure inside the nested walk is swallowed by `catch` with a warning and the include is dropped. Separately, a `sourceCode` over 500 characters is **silently truncated to 200** with `…[truncated]` appended — the masking shape applied to data |
| `GetTableContents` | inherits `GetSqlQuery`'s parse-failure masking: an unreadable document becomes empty `columns`/`rows` with `isError: false` |
| `GetPackageContents` | a throw is answered `{ isError: true, content: [{ text: message }] }` rather than through `return_error`, so an AxiosError's status and body are reduced to its `message`. Not an empty success, but the same loss of what SAP said |

From **compact** — three shared parsers, inherited by every tool that routes through them:

| parser or tool | the masking |
|---|---|
| `parseCheckRunResponse` (`src/lib/checkRunParser.ts:50`) | a document with no `chkrun:checkReport` returns `{ success: true, status: 'no_report', message: 'No check report in response (no issues reported)' }`. A body it could not read is reported as "no issues". Reaches `HandlerCheckRun` and all thirteen `Check*Low` and `Check*` rows |
| `parseValidationResponse` (`src/lib/utils.ts:1073`) | *"No data means validation passed"* — an `asx:abap/asx:values/DATA` it cannot find yields `{ valid: true }`. Reaches `HandlerValidate`, all fourteen `Validate*Low` and `ValidateServiceBinding` |
| `handleActivateObject` | computes `success = activated && checked` and answers through `return_response` **regardless**, so a failed activation reaches the caller as `isError: false` carrying `success: false`. Reaches `HandlerActivate`, `ActivateObjects` (high) and `ActivateObjectLow` |

From **high**:

| tool | the masking |
|---|---|
| `CreateFunctionGroup` | on a 400 mentioning "Kerberos library not loaded" or "Business partner", answers `success: true` with *"may have been created"* — **without verifying anything**. Its own comment: `// For now, we'll assume it was created and return success` |
| `CreateClass` | a non-empty `state.errors` still answers `success: true`, with the errors in the payload and `(with N error(s))` appended to the message |
| `CreateCdsUnitTest`, `UpdateCdsUnitTest` | the same, through `test_class_state.errors` |
| `CreateTransport` | `transportNumber` and `taskNumber` are declared and never assigned, and nothing checks that a number was parsed — so an unparsed body answers `success: true` with `transport_request: undefined` and the message `Transport request unknown created successfully` |
| all fourteen `Activate<X>` | `success = activated && checked` is written into the payload and the envelope stays `isError: false` either way |
| all fifteen `Delete<X>` | the deletion document is never read, so a 200 carrying `isDeleted="false"` and a `del:message` is answered as a successful delete |
| `GetCdsUnitTestResult`, `GetUnitTestResult`, `GetCdsUnitTestStatus`, `GetUnitTestStatus` | answer `success: true` with the result or status key **absent** when the read produced none — a caller cannot tell that from "the run had no findings" |
| `GetObjectsList` *(search group, listed here with its siblings)* | see the row below |

From **low**:

| tool | the masking |
|---|---|
| `ActivateClassTestClassesLow` | never calls `parseActivationResponse`. It takes the HTTP status and writes a **literal** `success: true`, so a 200 that refused the activation is a success and no message from the activation reaches the caller. The strongest case in the group |
| `CreatePackageLow`, `CreateFunctionModuleLow` | the create result is **never captured**, so `success: true` is written over a call nobody looked at |
| `UnlockFunctionModuleLow`, `UnlockClassTestClassesLow` | the same, for the unlock result |
| `UpdateClassTestClassesLow` | `updateResult` is read into `status` and `undefined` is acceptable, so an update that produced nothing still answers `success: true` |
| `UnlockTableLow` | validates nothing; a missing argument surfaces as a raw `TypeError` rather than as this server's own refusal |
| `CreateBehaviorImplementationLow` | the message asserts *"created and activated successfully"* and the handler performs no activation |
| `CreateTransportLow` | checks `createResult` exists and discards it, so the transport number is never read out — a success the caller cannot act on |
| all thirteen `Delete<X>Low` | the deletion document is never read. `DeleteFunctionModuleLow` is the case previously recorded as still open |
| all thirteen `Check<X>Low` | inherit `parseCheckRunResponse`'s `no_report` → `success: true` |
| all fourteen `Validate<X>Low` | inherit `parseValidationResponse`'s *"No data means validation passed"*; and `success` is `result.valid`, so a failed validation is `isError: false` with `success: false` |
| all fourteen `Activate<X>Low` and `ActivateObjectLow` | `success` in the payload, `isError: false` either way |

**Silent truncation is the same defect applied to data**, and the design says so — *"Silent
truncation is not an option — it is the masking problem applied to data."* Three cases:

| tool | the truncation |
|---|---|
| `GetObjectsList` | `parseValidObjects` emits only nodes carrying all four of `OBJECT_TYPE`, `OBJECT_NAME`, `TECH_NAME`, `OBJECT_URI`; a node missing any one is **dropped with no marker** (`handleGetObjectsList.ts:56`). The whole parse loop is wrapped in `catch (_error) {}` (65-67), so a throw part-way through returns the nodes collected so far as if they were all of them |
| `GetEnhancements` | source over 500 characters cut to 200 with `…[truncated]`, and includes dropped from the nested walk on failure |
| `DescribeByList` | objects dropped from the answer by an empty `catch {}` |

**And one found by measurement rather than by reading** (see *The traversal bound*):

| tool | the masking |
|---|---|
| `GetPackageContents`, `GetObjectsList` | a package that **does not exist** is answered `[]` / `total_objects: 0` with `isError: false` — measured against `ZMCP_SHR_PKG` on the trial system, where `GetPackageTree` correctly refused with `Package ZMCP_SHR_PKG not found`. A non-existent package is indistinguishable from an empty one on two of the three walking tools |


## The traversal bound

**Measured, not guessed.** Five packages on the SAP BTP ABAP trial system named by
`tests/test-config.yaml` (destination `trial`, client 100, `isLegacy=false`), on 2026-09-10.

```bash
npx tsx scripts/probe-package-contents.ts --env ~/.config/mcp-abap-adt/sessions/trial.env \
  --package <PKG> [--max-depth 99]
```

Round trips are counted by wrapping **`getAxiosInstance()`**, the single funnel every HTTP
call goes through — including `fetchCsrfToken`'s GET and the 403-retry path, which
`makeAdtRequest` never sees. `connect()` is awaited before instrumenting, so the one-off
discovery/CSRF handshake is not charged to whichever tool ran first. `GetPackageTree`'s totals
include the one existence pre-check its handler makes before walking.

**"count" is a different metric per tool** — tree nodes, flat-list entries, and
`total_objects` respectively — so compare a tool to itself on another package, never to its
neighbours.

| package | tool | round trips | count | ms |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ZADT_BLD_PKG03` (2 subpackages, 4 objects) | `GetPackageTree` | **7** | 7 nodes | 5462 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (default, one level) | **4** | 6 entries | 2482 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (recursive) | **6** | 6 entries | 5276 |
| | `GetObjectsList` | **7** | 8 objects | 4308 |
| `ZABAPCLOUD01` | `GetPackageTree` | **7** | 11 nodes | 5024 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (default) | **2** | 3 entries | 1424 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (recursive) | **6** | 10 entries | 3725 |
| | `GetObjectsList` | **4** | 4 objects | 2477 |
| `Z192_RAP` | `GetPackageTree` | **13** | 15 nodes | 8582 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (default) | **12** | 14 entries | 7361 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (recursive) | **12** | 14 entries | 7597 |
| | `GetObjectsList` | **23** | 14 objects | 14767 |
| `ZABAP_RAP` (largest measured) | `GetPackageTree` | **17** | 43 nodes | 11187 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (default) | **16** | 42 entries | 9591 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (recursive) | **16** | 42 entries | 10061 |
| | `GetObjectsList` | **31** | 42 objects | 18798 |
| `ZMCP_SHR_PKG` (**does not exist on this system**) | `GetPackageTree` | 1 | — refused | 548 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (default) | 1 | **0 entries, `isError: false`** | 579 |
| | `GetPackageContents` (recursive) | 1 | **0 entries, `isError: false`** | 536 |
| | `GetObjectsList` | 1 | **0 objects, `isError: false`** | 564 |

### What the numbers say

- **Latency is ~600 ms per round trip on this system**, steady across all four tools
  (11187/17 = 658; 18798/31 = 606; 8582/13 = 660). The bound is therefore a wall-clock bound
  in disguise, and that is the number a caller feels.
- **Cost scales with what is returned, roughly one round trip per one to three items** for
  the package tools.
- **`GetObjectsList` is the expensive one**: 23 round trips for 14 objects on `Z192_RAP`,
  1.6 per object, because it expands node ids rather than packages. This is exactly why the
  design refuses it a `depth` and gives it a frontier instead.
- **`max_depth: 99` changed nothing** on `ZADT_BLD_PKG03` — identical 7/4/6/7 — because that
  tree is two levels deep. Depth is not the binding constraint on a shallow package; the
  request count is.

### The bound

**200 round trips, per call, for all three tools.**

The reasoning, so it can be argued with rather than inherited:

- It is **≈6× the largest real package measured** (31), so nothing in the sampled range is
  truncated, and a package an order of magnitude larger than `ZABAP_RAP` still completes.
- At the measured 0.6 s per round trip it caps one call at **≈2 minutes**. That is long, but
  it is the honest ceiling for a tool that walks; a caller who cannot wait passes `depth`,
  which is what the parameter is for.
- It is one number for all three rather than three, because the unit — a round trip — is the
  same for all three and the counts are not. A per-tool bound would have to be justified per
  tool, and nothing in the measurement asks for that yet.
- **It is not a truncation.** When the bound stops a walk the payload says so
  (`stopped_at_bound`) and carries what was not expanded — package names for the two package
  tools, frontier node ids for `GetObjectsList` — so the answer is continuable and never
  silently partial.

**What would move it.** These five packages are small; the largest customer root package on a
real system may be far larger. If a later measurement shows a realistic package exceeding 200
round trips, the bound moves — and because a bounded answer says so, that will surface as a
`stopped_at_bound: true` a caller reports, not as a silent short list.

### A masking case the measurement found

`ZMCP_SHR_PKG`, which `tests/test-config.yaml` names as the shared-objects package, **does not
exist on this trial system**. The four tools disagreed about that:

- `GetPackageTree` **refused** — `Package ZMCP_SHR_PKG not found` — which is its
  `// Verify package exists before building tree (fixes #38)` pre-check earning its round trip,
  observed rather than inferred.
- `GetPackageContents` answered `[]` with `isError: false`, twice.
- `GetObjectsList` answered `total_objects: 0` with `isError: false`.

So a package that does not exist is indistinguishable from an empty one on two of the three
tools. That is the masking shape, and it is added to the masking table above. The fix is the
one `GetPackageTree` already implements: a walk that cannot find its root refuses instead of
returning nothing.

## `response_format` on the service-binding tools — an open decision

**Not settled here, and it must be settled before the service-binding rows are migrated.**
This has the same standing as the traversal bound: a decision the design leaves to stage 1,
recorded so it is visible rather than discovered.

Four tools take `response_format`: `GetServiceBinding`, `DeleteServiceBinding`,
`UpdateServiceBinding` and `ListServiceBindingTypes` (plus `HandlerServiceBindingListTypes`,
which passes it through). It looks like a verbosity knob on the same document, which is the
axis `detail` owns — and by the rule applied to `GetObjectsByType`'s `format` and
`RuntimeGetDumpById`'s `response_mode`, a knob on that axis is removed and subsumed.

**It is not the same, and that is why it is not removed.** `response_format` also decides the
`Accept` header sent to ADT, so it selects **which document SAP returns**, not merely how much
of one document is shown. Removing it would either pin the `Accept` header to one value — a
behaviour change for every caller that passes the other — or leave `detail` silently deciding
a request header, which is worse: `detail` is documented as choosing how much of an answer to
show, and a caller reading that would not expect it to change what was asked for.

The three ways out, none of which this table may choose on its own:

| option | consequence |
|---|---|
| keep `response_format` alongside `detail` | two knobs, but on genuinely different axes — one picks the document, the other how much of it. The pair is only confusing if the descriptions do not say so |
| remove it and pin the `Accept` header | simplest surface; a breaking change for callers using the non-default format, which needs a `parameter_removed` refusal and a release note |
| rename it to something that reads as a request option (`accept_format`) | keeps the behaviour, removes the collision with `detail`'s vocabulary; still a schema-breaking rename |

Until it is settled, the four rows carry `response_format` unchanged and say so.

## Findings about the handlers

Recorded, not corrected — this task writes one document and touches no `src/`.

### `GetObjectInfo`'s `enrich` has never worked

`enrichNodeWithSearchObject` (`handleGetObjectInfo.ts:82-87`) calls

```ts
const searchResult = await handleSearchObject(context, {
  query: objectName, object_type: objectType, maxResults: 1,
});
```

`handleSearchObject` destructures `object_name` and answers
`return_error('object_name is required')` when it is absent — so every enrichment call
returns `isError: true`, the `!searchResult.isError` gate below it is false, and the loop
never runs. Even if the parameter name were fixed, the body would still fail: the function
feeds `entry.text` to an `XMLParser` and looks for `adtcore:objectReferences`, while
`handleSearchObject` returns **tab-separated text**, not XML. Two independent defects, so
`OBJECT_DESCRIPTION` and `OBJECT_PACKAGE` are always `undefined` and `enrich` costs a
round trip per node for nothing.

This matters to the migration: a plan reading only the `TOOL_DEFINITION` would preserve
`enrich` as a working feature.

### Three inputs are accepted and never read

| tool | input | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `GetAdtTypes` | `validate_type` | the handler signature is `(context, _args)`; nothing validates a type. The description advertises "or validate a specific type name" |
| `GetPackageTree` | `debug` | declared in the schema with a description promising "diagnostic metadata in response (counts, types, hierarchy info)"; `args.debug` appears nowhere in the body |
| `GetSession` | `force_new` | read into a local and used only to decorate a debug log line; no branch depends on it |

### `GetSession` returns `session_state: null` while 100+ tools ask for it

`handleGetSession.ts:69` hardcodes `session_state: null` with the comment *"Session state
management is now handled by auth-broker"*. Every `*Low` tool's schema still describes
`session_state` as *"Session state from GetSession (cookies, csrf_token, cookie_store).
Required if session_id is provided."* The documented handshake cannot be completed with
what `GetSession` returns.

### `objectsListCache` is written in two incompatible shapes

`GetTypeInfo`, `GetWhereUsed` and `GetObjectsByType` call `objectsListCache.setCache()`
with a whole **MCP result object** (`{ isError, content }`). `GetObjectsList` calls it with
`{ parent_name, parent_tech_name, parent_type, total_objects, objects }`.
`GetObjectNodeFromCache` reads `cache.objects` — which only the second shape has. A cache
last written by any of the first three makes `GetObjectNodeFromCache` answer
`Node not found in cache` regardless of what is in it.

### Content items of type `json`, and results with no `isError`

Six handlers return `content: [{ type: 'json', json: … }]` — a content item type the MCP
content schema does not define: `GetObjectNodeFromCache`, `GetTypeInfo`, `GetWhereUsed`,
`GetObjectInfo`, `GetAbapSystemSymbols`, `GetTransaction`.

Two omit `isError` from the success result entirely: `GetObjectNodeFromCache` and
`GetObjectsList`. `GetObjectsList` additionally returns a top-level `cache` key beside
`content`, which is not part of an MCP result.

### `GetObjectInfo` is a fourth unbounded-ish traversal

The design names three traversals to bound (`GetPackageTree`, `GetPackageContents`,
`GetObjectsList`). `GetObjectInfo` also recurses — `buildTree` calls itself per group node
— but is already bounded by `maxDepth` (default 2 for `PROG/*` and `FUGR/*`, 1 otherwise).
It is not unbounded, so it needs no new bound; it is recorded so the migration does not
discover it as a surprise.

## Findings about the skeleton

None for this group. Every system and search row's implementation file, group and input
list matched the handler as read.

## Table — system and search (34 rows)

| tool | group | implementation files | visible in | current inputs | current default output | transformation today | error decision today | guarantees | new inputs | new terse projection | dropped | justification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DescribeByList | system | `src/handlers/system/readonly/handleDescribeByList.ts` | readonly, readonly,high, readonly,low | objects* | `{ isError: false, content: [...] }`, one `{ type:'text', text: JSON.stringify({ name, results: [...] }) }` item per input object **that matched**; objects that did not match are absent. On the outer catch: `{ isError: true, content: [] }` — an error carrying no message | no parser of its own. Loops `objects`, calls `handleSearchObject` per entry (once with `type`, then again without it if the first answer was empty or `isError`), `JSON.parse`s each returned content item's `text` and concatenates the `results` arrays | missing / empty / non-array `objects` → throws a hand-built `Error` with `status = 400` and `body.code = 'INVALID_PARAMS'`, which **escapes the handler unwrapped**. Nothing else is a failure: a per-object failure is swallowed by `catch {}` and the object is dropped; the outer catch answers `isError: true` with an empty `content`. **Masking** — see the table above | rejects a missing, empty or non-array `objects` before any request. Retries each object **without** `object_type` when the typed search came back empty or errored, so a wrong type does not silently yield nothing | objects*, +detail | `{ described: [{ name, results: [{ name, type, description, package }] }], not_described: [name] }` | nothing from today's payload | the shape is unchanged; `not_described` is **added**, because dropping an object with no marker is precisely the masking shape this migration exists to remove |
| GetAbapAST | system | `src/handlers/system/readonly/handleGetAbapAST.ts` | readonly, readonly,high, readonly,low | code* | `{ isError:false, content:[{ type:'text', text: JSON.stringify(ast, null, 2) }] }`, `ast = { type:'abapSource', sourceLength, lineCount, structures[], includes[], classes[], methods[], dataDeclarations[], forms[] }` | **no ADT call at all.** `SimpleAbapASTGenerator.parseToAST` scans the string the caller passed with regexes and line prefixes; the class comments itself as "a placeholder implementation until ANTLR is properly configured" | missing `code` → `return_error('ABAP code is required')`. A throw from the generator → `{ isError:true, content:[{type:'text', text: message}] }`. There is no SAP call, so no ADT error path exists | requires `code`. Nothing else | code*, +detail | `{ lineCount, structures, includes, classes, methods, forms }` | `type`, `sourceLength`, `dataDeclarations` | `type` is the constant `'abapSource'`; `sourceLength` restates the length of the string the caller sent; `dataDeclarations` is a regex over `DATA:` yielding local variable names, which address nothing on the server. `includes` **stays** — an include name is an object name the caller can read next. **This tool never reaches the adapter**: it makes no client call, so it keeps `return_error` / a direct result per the design's "paths that do not go through a client member" |
| GetAbapSemanticAnalysis | system | `src/handlers/system/readonly/handleGetAbapSemanticAnalysis.ts` | readonly, readonly,high, readonly,low | code* | `{ isError:false, content:[{ type:'text', text: JSON.stringify(analysis, null, 2) }] }`, `analysis = { symbols: [{ name, type, scope, line, column, description?, package?, visibility?, dataType?, parameters? }], dependencies: string[], errors: [{ message, line, column, severity }], scopes: [{ name, type, startLine, endLine, parent? }] }` | **no ADT call.** `SimpleAbapSemanticAnalyzer.analyze` over the caller's string; same placeholder lineage as `GetAbapAST` | missing `code` → `return_error('ABAP code is required')`. A throw → `{ isError:true, content:[{type:'text', text: message}] }`. No ADT error path | requires `code`. ## Findings — readonly

### `GetEnhancements` ignores a required input and reads five undeclared ones

Its schema requires `object_name` **and `object_type`**. The handler never reads
`object_type`: `determineObjectTypeAndPath` decides the type itself by probing
`/oo/classes/{n}`, then `/programs/programs/{n}`, then `/programs/includes/{n}` and taking
the first that answers 200. So a caller must pass a value that changes nothing, and a
caller who passes the *wrong* value is not corrected — it was never consulted.

In the other direction the handler reads five keys the schema does not declare, each of
which changes behaviour: `args.program` (manual parent-program context for an include),
`args.include_nested` (recurse into every include), `args.detailed` (full output rather
than the filtered one), `args.timeout` (per-request timeout, default 30000) and
`args.max_includes` (cap on includes processed, default 50). Depending on how the MCP
layer treats unknown properties, these are either unreachable or reachable-but-undocumented;
either way the tool's public surface and its behaviour disagree.

`GetIncludesList` has a smaller version of the same defect: `detailed` is declared —
*"If true, returns structured JSON with metadata and raw XML"* — and never read.

### Sixteen `Read*` handlers answer `{ success: true, source_code: null }`

`ReadBehaviorDefinition`, `ReadBehaviorImplementation`, `ReadClass`, `ReadDataElement`,
`ReadDdl`, `ReadDomain`, `ReadFunctionGroup`, `ReadFunctionInclude`, `ReadInterface`,
`ReadMetadataExtension`, `ReadPackage`, `ReadServiceBinding`, `ReadServiceDefinition`,
`ReadStructure`, `ReadTable` — and `ReadMessageClass` / `ReadMessageClassMessage` in their
own shape — all follow one template:

```ts
let source_code: string | null = null;
const readResult = await obj.read(…);
if (readResult?.readResult?.data) { source_code = …; }
…
return return_response({ data: JSON.stringify({ success: true, …, source_code, metadata }) });
```

A read that produced no `data` is therefore delivered as `success: true` with
`source_code: null`. That is the **read-masking** defect this repository already fixed once,
still present in seventeen tools. **`ReadFunctionModule` is one of them** — its `containerRef`
guarantee protects the *group*, and once that check passes the same null-bearing success
follows, which its own row says. `ReadProgram` is the only exception and shows what the fix
looks like: it inspects the metadata and answers `success: false, error: 'invalid_object_type'`
rather than a null-bearing success — and its own comment says it is doing exactly that.

This is the single largest reason the readonly group is worth migrating first.

### `GetEnhancementImpl` answers "not found" as a success

When the enhancement URL does not return 200-with-data, the handler makes a **second**
request for the spot and answers `isError: false` with
`{ status: 'not_found', message: …, spot_metadata }`. Unlike the sixteen above it names the
condition in a field, so a caller *can* branch — but it is still a refusal delivered as a
success, and the field is not one any other tool uses.

### Two traversals in this group already report their own bounds

`GetIncludesList` walks the include tree to `MAX_INCLUDE_DEPTH = 20`, marking a node
`truncated: true` at the limit and `cyclic: true` when it revisits a name.
`GetStructuresList` walks embeds/appends with a `visited` set, marks `cyclic`, records
`error` on a node whose source it could not read, and sets `appends_unavailable: true` when
the where-used lookup failed — its comment: *"Do NOT swallow the failure into an empty
result — flag the response as degraded so callers can tell 'could not determine appends'
apart from 'no appends'."* Both are the pattern the design asks the three package/node
traversals to adopt, already implemented here.

### Findings about the skeleton

None for this group. Every readonly row's implementation file, group and input list matched
the handler as read. Note that the skeleton's *current inputs* for `GetEnhancements` is
`object_name*, object_type*`, which is correct — it reports the schema, and the schema is
what the finding above is about.

| tool | group | implementation files | visible in | current inputs | current default output | transformation today | error decision today | guarantees | new inputs | new terse projection | dropped | justification |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Otherwise the spot fallback: `{ isError:false, content:[{ type:'json', json: { enhancement_spot, enhancement_name, status:'not_found', message, spot_metadata: { description? } } }] }` | `parseEnhancementSourceFromXml` — tries a base64 `<source>`/`<enh:source>` element and `Buffer.from(…, 'base64')`, then a CDATA form, and **returns the whole XML unchanged** if neither matches. The spot fallback pulls only `<adtcore:description>` with one regex | missing either name → `return_error`. `status !== 200` or falsy data → the spot fallback, answered as `isError:false`. Both the impl request and the spot fallback failing → `return_error` naming both statuses. On "not found" it pays a second request to say **why** it could not answer, rather than answering an empty source | enhancement_spot*, enhancement_name*, +detail | `{ enhancement_spot, enhancement_name, source_code }` | `status`, `message`, `spot_metadata` | the `not_found` branch stops being a projection at all: it becomes a **failure**, so its three fields move into the failure payload's `message`. | GetEnhancements | readonly | `src/handlers/enhancement/readonly/handleGetEnhancements.ts` | readonly, readonly,high, readonly,low | object_name*, object_type* | default (`detailed` falsy, `include_nested` falsy): `{ object_name, object_type, context, detailed:false, total_enhancements, enhancements: [{ name, type, sourceCode }] }` — each `sourceCode` **truncated to 200 characters with `…[truncated]`** when it exceeds 500. With `include_nested`: `{ main_object:{name,type}, include_nested:true, detailed:false, total_objects_with_enhancements, total_objects_analyzed, filtered_out, objects:[…] }`. On a nested failure: a **partial** payload with `partial_result:true` and an `error` string, and **no `isError` key at all** | `parseEnhancementsFromXml` — regexes over `<enh:source>` with base64 decode, plus four fallback patterns to guess each enhancement's name and three to guess its type; a name it cannot find becomes `enhancement_1`, `enhancement_2`, … Then `filterMinimalEnhancements` truncates | missing `object_name` → `return_error`. `determineObjectTypeAndPath` failing all three probes → `EnhancementInputError` → the outer catch → `return_error`. A non-200 from the enhancements URL → `EnhancementInputError` → `return_error`. A **per-include** failure inside the nested walk is swallowed by `catch` with a warning and the include is dropped. Nested processing failing wholesale → the `partial_result` payload, `isError` absent | `EnhancementInputError` exists so the helpers' catch blocks can tell *"I decided this input is unusable"* from *"something unexpected failed"* and avoid double-wrapping — quoted from its own doc comment. An include with no `contextRef` is refused by name rather than being requested without context. `maxIncludes` caps the nested walk at 50 and logs when it truncates; each nested request has its own timeout via `createPromiseWithTimeout`, which clears its timer on both settle paths | object_name*, +detail, and — **as a decision, not a discovery** — the five undeclared inputs `program`, `include_nested`, `detailed`, `timeout`, `max_includes` must either be **declared** or **removed**; they cannot stay undeclared-and-live. `object_type` **removed** — refused by name, `message: "object_type was never read; GetEnhancements determines the type itself"`. `detailed` is subsumed by `detail` | `{ object_name, object_type, enhancements: [{ name, type, sourceCode }], truncated_includes?: [name] }` | `context`, `detailed`, `total_enhancements`, `filtered_out`, `total_objects_analyzed`, `total_objects_with_enhancements` | `detailed` is replaced by `detail`. `context` is the internal URL the include lookup used. The four counters are `length`s of arrays in the same payload. **The 200-character truncation must not survive**: silently cutting source and appending `…[truncated]` is the masking shape applied to data — under `terse` the full `sourceCode` is carried, and a caller who wants less asks for less. `truncated_includes` is added for the includes the nested walk dropped |

The observation behind it stands — this object has no meaningful "source", and its identity lives in the metadata — so the answer is to **parse the metadata and project named fields**, not to hand over the document. The parse follows the design's `parseTransportTree` precedent: the structure is named, SAP's own attribute keys are kept verbatim, and `full` carries the whole parse. `source_code` is dropped because a data element has none — the read returns the same DDIC document the metadata call does. `type_name` is the domain this element points at, an object the caller reads next; `package` is what a sibling create needs. `success` per the standing rule; `version` echoes the caller's argument. The first pass proposed keeping the metadata **string** in `terse`, which would have made `terse` for this tool bulkier than `full` is for most others. The observation behind it stands — this object has no meaningful "source", and its identity lives in the metadata — so the answer is to **parse the metadata and project named fields**, not to hand over the document. The parse follows the design's `parseTransportTree` precedent: the structure is named, SAP's own attribute keys are kept verbatim, and `full` carries the whole parse. `value_table` names another object; `fixed_values` is the value range, which is the whole point of a domain and cannot be seen in any "source". `success` and `version` per the standing rules |

## Findings — compact

### The five "dead" handlers the skeleton found are not dead code

Task 1a recorded five files under `src/handlers/common/low/` whose `TOOL_DEFINITION` no
group registers — `handleCheckObject`, `handleDeleteObject`, `handleLockObject`,
`handleUnlockObject`, `handleValidateObject` — and concluded they are "dead, deliberately
disabled handlers". That is right about the **tools** and wrong about four of the five
**handler functions**: the compact group calls them directly.

| unregistered file | called by |
|---|---|
| `handleActivateObject` (registered as `ActivateObjectLow`) | `HandlerActivate` |
| `handleCheckObject` | `HandlerCheckRun` |
| `handleLockObject` | `HandlerLock` |
| `handleUnlockObject` | `HandlerUnlock` |
| `handleValidateObject` | `HandlerValidate`, and `handleDeleteObject` — genuinely unused |

So `handleDeleteObject` is the only one of the five that nothing reaches. The other four are
the implementation of four compact tools, are on the live path in the `compact` exposition,
and must be migrated with it. A migration plan acting on Task 1a's wording alone would have
deleted them.

### The compact group is 22 delegations and almost no logic of its own

Every compact tool is a thin adapter over one or more `high`, `low` or `system` handlers.
Its **output is its delegate's output, unchanged** — no compact handler reshapes a result.
What each one does add is:

- a **name mapping**, `CompactObjectType` (`CLASS`, `PROGRAM`, …) → the delegate's own
  vocabulary, via `compactRouterMap` for CRUD and `toLowObjectType` for the lifecycle four;
- a **refusal** when the pair is not in the map.

That has one consequence the migration must not miss: **`terse` for a compact tool is the
delegate's `terse`.** Twenty-two rows below therefore say "as `<delegate>`", and the
projection decision lives in the delegate's row, not here. Writing a second, independent
projection for the compact surface would be how the two drift apart.

### Compact refusals are thrown, not returned

`HandlerActivate`, `HandlerCheckRun`, `HandlerLock`, `HandlerUnlock`, `HandlerValidate` and
`HandlerProfileRun` raise a bare `throw new Error(...)` for an unsupported `object_type` or
a missing conditional argument, rather than answering `return_error`. These do reach the
caller as errors — `return_error`'s own comment confirms *"both registration paths route any
thrown error through here"* — but they take the handler's own error shaping out of the loop.
Under the new design a `throw` inside a handler is not covered by `answer`'s boundary either,
since it happens before the call; each of these six needs an explicit refusal.

### `compactRouterMap` is checked against `COMPACT_CRUD_MATRIX` at module load

`validateCompactRouterAgainstMatrix()` runs at import time and **throws on any mismatch**,
so a router entry added without a matrix entry (or the reverse) fails the process at startup
rather than producing an "Unsupported operation" at call time. This is a guarantee about the
tool surface itself and it is cheap to lose in a refactor.

### Three shared parsers mask a refusal, and the compact group inherits all three

Named here because the compact group is where all three meet, though they belong to
`src/lib/`:

| parser | the masking |
|---|---|
| `parseCheckRunResponse` (`src/lib/checkRunParser.ts:50`) | a document with no `chkrun:checkReport` returns `{ success: true, status: 'no_report', message: 'No check report in response (no issues reported)' }`. A body it could not read is reported as "no issues" |
| `parseValidationResponse` (`src/lib/utils.ts:1073`) | *"No data means validation passed"* — an `asx:abap/asx:values/DATA` it cannot find yields `{ valid: true }` |
| `parseActivationResponse` (`src/lib/utils.ts:2185`) | the one that does **not** mask: a parse failure returns `activated: false` with an `error` message. It is the model the other two need |

`handleActivateObject` then computes `success = activated && checked` and answers through
`return_response` **regardless** — so a failed activation reaches the caller as `isError:
false` carrying `success: false`. That is the activation-masking shape, on the
`activateObjectsGroup` path rather than the `activateObjectInSession` path fixed in 8.10.0.

### Findings about the skeleton

None for this group. The 22 compact rows' files, groups and input lists matched.

## Findings — high

### One rule this group needs before its rows can be read

The design fixes `terse` for a **write** at `SUCCESS`. That overrides the "a normalised
argument stays" convention stated at the top for this group: an uppercased echo of the name
the caller itself passed is not worth defeating the design's one explicit `terse` rule.
Where a write produces a value the caller **did not** have — a transport number, a `run_id`,
a service name SAP filled in, a function group resolved from metadata — that value stays.
Every write row below follows exactly that, and says which case it is.

### Two tools exist only to refuse, and that is correct

`DeleteUnitTest` and `UpdateUnitTest` make **no client call at all**. Each validates
`run_id` and then answers `return_error` with `Cannot delete/update unit test run <id>: ADT
does not support deleting/updating test runs.` This is the right shape — a caller learns the
operation does not exist rather than discovering it from a 404 — and it is the exact mirror
of `GetTransaction` in the system group, which answers a success it cannot make good on.
Both should gain `code: 'UNSUPPORTED_OPERATION'` when migrated so a caller can branch
without matching prose, and both are candidates for removal from the surface, since a tool
that can only fail still costs a schema slot in every listing.

### Four defects that must be fixed by the migration, not carried

**This is a ruling, not a preference.** The whole point of the injected strategies is that a
handler must not assert an outcome it did not check: `analyse` sees the same endpoint response
the result strategy sees, and it decides and states whether the call failed. A handler that
guesses is the thing being removed. So these four do not travel:

| defect | what the migration must do instead |
|---|---|
| **`CreateFunctionGroup`** answers `success: true` with *"may have been created"* on a Kerberos or business-partner 400, its comment saying `// For now, we'll assume it was created` | **verify or fail.** Read the group back — `CreateFunctionGroupLow` already does exactly this on its own 400, with `read({ functionGroupName }, 'inactive', { withLongPolling: true })`, and answers success only when the read returns a `readResult`. Where the read fails, the original error reaches the caller |
| **`CreateClass`** returns every field twice, once inside `data` and once beside it, and answers `success: true` when `state.errors` is non-empty | **one payload, and errors are a failure.** Drop the duplicated `data` object — no caller can know which copy is authoritative. A non-empty `state.errors` makes the call `isError: true` with those errors in the failure payload, which is what the design's message rule already says: every message reaches the caller, and an `E` raises the failure. The same applies to `CreateCdsUnitTest` and `UpdateCdsUnitTest` through `test_class_state.errors` |
| **`RunUnitTest` and `CreateUnitTest`** are the same handler under two names — identical inputs, identical uppercasing, identical `run_id`-or-throw check, identical payload down to the message text | **one implementation, and say which name is canonical.** Both are registered, so both rows exist in this table; the migration implements one handler and points the second name at it, rather than writing the same projection twice and letting them drift |
| **`GetObjectInfo`'s `enrich`** has never worked — the call passes `query` where `handleSearchObject` requires `object_name`, and the body then feeds tab-separated text to an `XMLParser` | **remove the parameter by name.** `OBJECT_DESCRIPTION` and `OBJECT_PACKAGE` are always `undefined`, so nothing is lost; keeping the field and the input would preserve the appearance of a feature that has never run. If enrichment is wanted it is new behaviour with its own row and its own review |

Each of the four has a row in the table below whose `justification` cell says the same thing.
None of them is optional, and none of them is this table's invention: they are what the
contract the design defines makes impossible to keep.

### `CreateFunctionGroup` returns a success it has not verified

`src/handlers/function/high/handleCreateFunctionGroup.ts:311-338`. On HTTP 400 whose body or
message mentions **"Kerberos library not loaded"** or **"Business partner"**, the handler
answers `success: true` with the message *"Function group X may have been created
successfully (SAP returned error but object exists)"*. Its own comment is explicit:

```
// Check if object was actually created by trying to get it
// For now, we'll assume it was created and return success
// This is a known issue with FunctionGroup creation in SAP
```

This is the masking defect written down and shipped. `CreatePackage` shows what the same
situation looks like when done properly: on HTTP 404 with *"Error while importing object"* it
**reads the package back** and answers success only if the read succeeded, adding
`warning: 'Import warning during create (404). Object verified by read.'`. The
`CreateFunctionGroup` branch must not be ported as-is — either it verifies, or the error
reaches the caller.

### `CreateClass` returns every field twice, and reports its own errors as a success

`handleCreateClass` builds `{ success: true, data: { … }, …the same five fields again… }` —
`class_name`, `package_name`, `transport_request`, `activated` and `errors` appear both
inside `data` and beside it. No other handler does this, and no caller can know which copy is
authoritative.

Separately, when `state.errors` is non-empty the answer is still `success: true`, with the
errors in the payload and `(with N error(s))` appended to the message. `CreateCdsUnitTest`
and `UpdateCdsUnitTest` have the same shape through `test_class_state.errors`. This is the
create-side of the masking family: three fixes have closed read, deletion and activation, and
this is the fourth face of the same defect.

### `activate` defaults disagree across the group, invisibly

| defaults to `true` | defaults to `false` |
|---|---|
| `UpdateBehaviorDefinition`, `UpdateBehaviorImplementation`, `UpdateInterface`, `UpdateServiceDefinition`, `UpdateStructure`, `UpdateTable`, `UpdateDomain`, `UpdateDataElement`, `UpdateMetadataExtension`, `CreateBehaviorDefinition`, `CreateMetadataExtension`, `CreateDomain`, `CreateDataElement`, `CreateFunctionGroup`, `CreateServiceBinding` | `UpdateClass`, `UpdateProgram`, `UpdateDdl`, `UpdateFunctionModule`, `UpdateFunctionInclude`, and every `Update<X>Local*` via `activate_on_update` |

`UpdateClass` and `UpdateInterface` are the two most similar tools in the repository and
they disagree: `args.activate === true` versus `activate !== false`. The schemas describe
neither default. Nothing in this table changes it — but a migration touching all of these
files at once is the moment the divergence either gets settled or gets frozen for good.

### The high group's own guarantees, beyond the brief's two

Found by reading the bodies, and each is a reason a field or a round trip stays:

- **`Get<X>` refuses an empty read; `Read<X>` does not.** Every one of the fourteen
  `Get<X>` handlers opens with `if (!readResult || !readResult.readResult) throw new
  Error('<X> not found')`. The sixteen `readonly` `Read<X>` handlers have no such check and
  answer `success: true, source_code: null`. Two families, one operation, opposite
  behaviour on the same failure.
- **`GetFunctionModule` carries the same `containerRef` ownership check as
  `ReadFunctionModule`** — the brief named only the readonly one. Metadata is read first,
  an absent `containerRef` is a refusal, and the source is fetched with the group the
  metadata named, not the caller's.
- **`UpdateClass`, `UpdateProgram`, `UpdateDdl`, `UpdateInterface`, `UpdateStructure` and
  `UpdateTable` check the new code before writing it**, and abort if the check fails — so an
  object is never overwritten with source that does not compile. The check is skipped when
  `activate` is false, which each of them logs.
- **`UpdateFunctionGroup` reads the current metadata inside the same locked session before
  writing**, so the update cannot drop a field it did not know about.
- **`UpdateFunctionInclude` and `UpdateFunctionModule` translate HTTP 400 with no
  `transport_request` into "the object may be assigned to a transport request; pass
  `transport_request` explicitly"** — naming the one argument the caller probably omitted.
- **`CreatePackage` separates 401 from 403** — reauthenticate versus ask for a role. It is
  the only handler in the repository that does.
- **`GetLocalDefinitions` and `GetLocalTypes` put the failing URL and the first 800
  characters of the body into the HTTP 406 message**, because those includes are not
  readable on every system and a bare 406 would read as a bug in this server.
- **`CreateServiceDefinition` calls `update()` after `create()`** when `source_code` was
  given — the create-shell/update-body invariant, and the fix for the empty-body defect
  closed in core 8.7.1.
- **`CreateMessageClass` deliberately does not activate**, and says why in its header:
  message classes are not activated; individual messages are added afterwards.
- **The cleanup unlocks in `CreateDomain`, `CreateDataElement`, `UpdateDomain` and
  `UpdateDataElement` swallow their own error on purpose**, so the first failure is the one
  reported rather than being masked by a failure to clean up after it.
- **`buildObjectVersionTools`' `VERSIONED_TYPES` table excludes types with no version
  history** — quoted: *"their getVersions/getVersionSource throw 'not supported', so
  per-type version tools for them would always error"* — and copies each row's
  `available_in` from that type's own `Get<X>`, so the two cannot diverge.

### Two tools, one behaviour: `RunUnitTest` and `CreateUnitTest`

`src/handlers/unit_test/high/handleRunUnitTest.ts` and
`src/handlers/unit_test/high/handleCreateUnitTest.ts` are the same handler with two names:
identical inputs, identical uppercasing, identical `run_id`-or-throw check, identical
payload down to the message text. Both are registered. Every later plan will implement both.
Worth resolving before the migration doubles the work rather than after.

### Findings about the skeleton

None for this group. All 156 rows' files, groups and input lists matched the handlers as
read, including the fourteen `Activate*` renames resolved by hand in Task 1a — each of those
rows' second file is indeed the `low` handler the `high` group re-registers under a shorter
name, and the handler body is shared with the `*Low` row verbatim.

Splits `messages` into `warnings` and `errors` accepting **both** spellings SAP uses — `'warning'`/`'W'` and `'error'`/`'E'` | structure_name*, session_id, session_state, +detail | `SUCCESS` when the document carried no messages; otherwise `{ messages }` — every `W` and `I` message, since an `E` makes the call a failure and its messages travel in `getError().messages` | `success`, `structure_name`, `activation.activated`, `activation.checked`, `activation.generated`, `warnings`, `errors`, `session_id`, `session_state`, `message` | `success` **becomes `isError`** — that is the masking fix, not a projection choice. `activated` and `checked` are what decided it, so they belong with the verdict rather than beside it; `generated` describes what just happened. `warnings` and `errors` are `messages` re-filtered, and the split is the error strategy's job once an `E` is present. `session_state` is the constant `null`; `session_id` is an echo (this handler never mints one); `message` is prose |
| ActivateTable | high | `src/lib/handlers/groups/HighLevelHandlersGroup.ts`, `src/handlers/table/low/handleActivateTable.ts` | high, readonly,high | table_name*, session_id, session_state | `{ success, table_name, activation: { activated, checked, generated }, messages: [{ type, text, line?, column? }], warnings, errors, session_id, session_state: null, message }` as an indented JSON string. `success = activated && checked`, and **the envelope is `isError: false` either way** | `parseActivationResponse` (`src/lib/utils.ts:2139`) — `fast-xml-parser` over `chkl:messages`, reading `@_activationExecuted`, `@_checkExecuted`, `@_generationExecuted` and each `msg`'s `@_type` and `shortText` | missing `table_name` → `return_error`. An activation that returned no response → `throw` → the inner catch. The inner catch rewrites HTTP 404 to `Table {name} not found.` and otherwise pulls `exc:exception/message` out of the body with a second `fast-xml-parser` pass, then `return_error`. **A refused activation is not a failure** — `success: false` travels inside `isError: false`. See the activation-masking note in Findings | uppercases `table_name`. Restores the caller's session when **both** `session_id` and `session_state` are given. Refuses an activation that returned no response at all (`throw new Error('Activation did not return a response for …')`) rather than reporting an empty success. Check if transport request is required and valid.`, and otherwise parses `exc:exception/message` out of the body — then `return_error`. **Deletion masking applies**: ADT answers 200 with `isDeleted="false"` and a `del:message`, and this handler never reads the document | uppercases `service_definition_name`. Refuses a delete that returned no response rather than reporting a successful deletion of nothing. ## Findings — low

**These columns were re-derived handler by handler in fix round 1.** The first pass composed
`guarantees` and `error decision today` from family templates and thereby asserted checks
that six handlers do not perform. Every cell in this group's last two columns now comes from
a per-handler record — validation, session restore, emptiness check, catch rewrites — read in
the file. Nothing is inherited from a sibling, and where a family really is uniform, every
member was read to prove it.

Two rules were applied:

- A guarantee is what the code **does**, quoted or cited. Inferred from a name, a comment or
  a sibling, it is not a guarantee.
- **Absence is a finding.** "No validation; a missing argument throws a raw `TypeError`" is a
  true and useful cell. An invented refusal is not.

### Six handlers assert nothing where the first pass said they refuse

Each of these was claimed to check something it does not. All six are now recorded as
absences, and five of them are **unmarked masking** — a success written over a result nobody
looked at.

| tool | what the first pass claimed | what the code does |
|---|---|---|
| `CreatePackageLow` | "refuses a create that returned no response" | `handleCreatePackage.ts:182` calls `await client.getPackage().create(createConfig)` **without capturing the result**; `success: true` is written unconditionally at 189 |
| `CreateFunctionModuleLow` | the same | `handleCreateFunctionModule.ts:48` likewise never captures the create result |
| `UnlockFunctionModuleLow` | "refuses an unlock that returned no response" | `handleUnlockFunctionModule.ts:47` awaits `unlock(...)` and never captures it |
| `UnlockClassTestClassesLow` | three behaviours at once — an emptiness check, a 400 rewrite, an `exc:exception` parse | it has none of the three. It validates `class_name` and `lock_handle`, calls `classClient.unlockTestClasses(...)` uncaptured, and its catch rewrites only 404 |
| `UnlockTableLow` | "requires the name and the handle together" | `handleUnlockTable.ts:75-90` validates **nothing**. `table_name.toUpperCase()` (87) and `session_id.substring(0, 8)` (90) run first, so a missing argument surfaces as a raw `TypeError` |
| `UpdateInterfaceLow` | an update with no response throws `Update did not return a response for …` | `handleUpdateInterface.ts:119` assigns `updateResult` and never reads it — **and the adjacent comment says the check would be wrong**: *"updateResult may be null for successful updates (204 No Content)"* |

`UpdateInterfaceLow` is the one that matters beyond bookkeeping: it is the counter-example
to "add the emptiness check everywhere". A 204 No Content is that endpoint's success, so the
write verdict must come from `ok` and the status, never from the presence of a body.

### Two templates that were true of almost nobody

**The `Check*Low` version clamp.** The first pass put *"clamps `version` to active/inactive,
falling back to 'active'"* on all thirteen rows. Read individually:

| rows | `version` |
|---|---|
| `CheckBdefLow`, `CheckDataElementLow`, `CheckDomainLow`, `CheckFunctionGroupLow`, `CheckInterfaceLow`, `CheckMetadataExtensionLow`, `CheckPackageLow`, `CheckProgramLow` | **no `version` input at all** — eight of thirteen |
| `CheckClassLow`, `CheckFunctionModuleLow` | default `'active'`, clamped to `['active','inactive']` |
| `CheckDdlLow`, `CheckStructureLow` | default **`'inactive'`** |
| `CheckTableLow` | default **`'new'`**, clamped to `['active','inactive','new']` |

Correct on two rows of thirteen. Each row now states its own default and its own enum.

**The `Validate*Low` "400-as-answer" template.** The first pass put it, plus a
`DEBUG_HANDLERS` claim, on all fourteen rows. Verified by reading each:

- **`ValidateClassLow`** captures `error.response` when its status is 400 and parses it. It
  is also the only handler in this group that reads `DEBUG_HANDLERS`
  (`handleValidateClass.ts:184`), using it to log an expected 400 at `debug` rather than
  `error`. (`DEBUG_HANDLERS` appears in one other handler, `handleCreateClass.ts:129`, where
  it gates a `canRefreshToken` debug line — nothing branches on it.)
- **`ValidateDataElementLow`** does it twice: an inner catch captures `validateError.response`
  whatever its status, and the outer catch has a second 400 branch that re-parses the body and
  returns a **success payload from the catch block**.
- **The other twelve let a 400 throw.** On those twelve, `exists: true` is unreachable and
  "already exists" is not reportable at all — the opposite of the claimed guarantee.

### Handlers that assert an outcome they never checked

Beyond the six above, the re-derivation found three more places where the answer states
something the code did not establish. Under the design these are exactly what `analyse` —
which sees the same endpoint response — exists to decide, so none may be carried over:

- **`ActivateClassTestClassesLow`** never calls `parseActivationResponse`. It awaits
  `classClient.activate({ className })`, takes the HTTP status, and writes a **literal**
  `success: true`. A 200 that refused the activation is reported as a success and not one
  message from the activation reaches the caller. Its `test_class_name` input is uppercased
  into a local and never used — the handler always activates the parent class.
- **`CreateBehaviorImplementationLow`**'s message reads *"Behavior Implementation <name>
  created and activated successfully."* — and no `activate` call appears anywhere in the
  handler. The same handler passes `implementation_code` into `create()` as `sourceCode`,
  which violates the create-shell/update-body invariant this repository fixed in core 8.7.1.
- **`CreateTransportLow`** checks that `createState.createResult` exists and then discards it,
  so the transport number it was created under is never read out. Its `high` counterpart
  returns one. A caller cannot quote what it just created — the one row in the whole table
  where `terse` **adds** a field rather than dropping any.

### `CreateFunctionGroupLow` verifies its recovery; `CreateFunctionGroup` guesses

Both handle a 400 that may still have created the object. The `low` one, on a body containing
`Interface SAPL` and `has not been created`, **reads the group back** with `withLongPolling`
and answers success only if the read returned a `readResult`, adding
`warning: 'Interface SAPL* not created during create (400). Object verified by read.'`. The
`high` one, on a Kerberos or business-partner message, returns success with *"may have been
created"* and a comment saying it assumes. Same repository, same situation — and the `low` one
is the model.

### Session handling is not uniform, and three of the divergences matter

- **`LockClassLow`, `UpdateClassLow` and `UnlockClassLow` take no session parameters at all**,
  while every other family's triple does. The most-written object type is the one whose lock
  cannot be driven inside a caller-supplied session through the `low` tools —
  `LockClassTestClassesLow` and its siblings, which operate on the same class, all do take them.
- **`CreateClassLow` declares `session_id` and `session_state` and never calls
  `restoreSessionInConnection`.** The arguments are accepted, echoed back, and do nothing.
- **`LockFunctionGroupLow`, `LockFunctionModuleLow` and `LockPackageLow` return
  `session_state: session_state || null`** — the caller's own argument handed back — while
  every other handler in the group returns the literal `null`. The first pass justified
  dropping the field as "the constant `null`" on all three, which is wrong for these three;
  the reason it is still dropped is that an echo is an echo.
- The `Unlock*Low` family restores on **`session_state` alone** in ten handlers and on
  **`session_id && session_state`** in three (`UnlockClassTestClassesLow`, `UnlockDdlLow`,
  `UnlockFunctionModuleLow`); `UnlockClassLow` restores nothing.

### Every Program tool refuses on cloud, before any request

Eight `low` rows open with `isCloudConnection()` and answer *"Programs are not available on
cloud systems"* without issuing a request: `ActivateProgramLow`, `CheckProgramLow`,
`CreateProgramLow`, `DeleteProgramLow`, `LockProgramLow`, `UnlockProgramLow`,
`UpdateProgramLow`, `ValidateProgramLow`. The first pass recorded none of them. It is a real
guarantee — a caller on BTP gets one sentence instead of an ADT 404 — and it is also the
runtime half of the `available_in: ['onprem','legacy']` declaration, so a migration must not
assume the schema alone carries it.

### Two field-name divergences a generic consumer will trip over

- `LockClassTestClassesLow` returns the handle under **`test_classes_lock_handle`**, not
  `lock_handle`. It is the only lock handler with a different key, and a caller wiring
  `Lock → Update → Unlock` generically will read `undefined`.
- `DeleteMetadataExtensionLow` reports the name under **`ddlxName`** — the only camelCase key
  in any handler payload in this repository.

Both vanish under `terse: SUCCESS`; both are notices that if anything downstream reads those
keys, the migration is where it breaks.

### `CheckTableLow`'s `reporter` is dead, and it does mint a session id

`checkReporter` is computed and validated against `['tableStatusCheck','abapCheckRun']` at
`handleCheckTable.ts:104-107` and then **never passed to `.check()`**, which is called as
`.check({ tableName, ddlCode }, checkVersion)`. The value reaches a log line and the answer's
own `reporter` field, and changes nothing about the check that runs. The first pass presented
this as a feature ("echoes the reporter it actually used").

The same handler **does** mint a session id — `session_id || generateSessionId()` at line 121,
returned as `session_id` — which the first pass denied. Its `terse` projection keeps it for
that reason.

### The low group's verified guarantees

Each of these was confirmed by reading every member of the family named, not one member and a
template:

- **All fifteen `Lock*Low` refuse an empty lock handle by name**
  (`throw new Error('Lock did not return a lock handle for …')`), which stops a caller
  proceeding with `lock_handle: undefined` into an update that would fail obscurely.
- **Twelve of the fourteen `Unlock*Low` rewrite HTTP 400** to *"Invalid lock handle. Make sure
  you're using the same lock_handle from Lock<X>."* — naming a stale or foreign handle.
  `UnlockClassTestClassesLow` rewrites only 404; `UnlockTableLow` does rewrite both but
  validates nothing first.
- **All thirteen `Update*Low` require an explicit `lock_handle`.** The low surface never locks
  on the caller's behalf, and that is what makes a lock/update/unlock sequence auditable.
- **`UpdateDataElementLow` refuses an unresolvable package before any request**, and strips
  keys whose value is `undefined` or `''` out of the update config, so an omitted property is
  left alone rather than written as empty.
- **`UpdateFunctionModuleLow` rewrites HTTP 400 with no `transport_request`** to *"The object
  may be assigned to a transport request. Pass transport_request explicitly."*
- **`CreatePackageLow` separates an expired token from a permission problem, and 401 from
  403** — the only handler in the repository that does both.
- **`ValidateFunctionModuleLow` checks the parse twice**, `if (!validationResponse) throw` and
  then `if (!result) throw`.
- **`RunClassUnitTestsLow` validates every entry of the batch and names the failing index**
  (`tests[i] must include container_class and test_class`), then refuses a run that produced
  no `run_id`, and reports the `Location` header as `location`.
- **`GetClassUnitTestResultLow` and `GetClassUnitTestStatusLow` refuse an absent response by
  name**, which their `high` siblings do not. `GetClassUnitTestStatusLow` also defaults
  `with_long_polling` to `true`, the opposite of its `high` sibling.
- **`DeletePackageLow`'s `force_new_connection` calls `connect()` explicitly**, because *"RFC
  connections require explicit connect() — createAbapConnection does not connect
  automatically"*, and falls back to the existing connection with a warning rather than
  failing the call.
- **`CreateFunctionModuleLow`'s post-create read is best-effort, not verification** — its catch
  is empty and commented *"Read is best-effort; create may still succeed without immediate
  read"*. It warms the object; it does not check it.

### `parseValidationResponse` earns its keep, and also masks

The same parser does both. On the exception branch it recognises
`ExceptionResourceAlreadyExists`, `InvalidClifName` with an "already exists" message, and
several message forms, and reports `exists: true` — the difference between renaming and
giving up, and a real reading no strategy should lose. On the standard branch it says *"No
data means validation passed"* and returns `{ valid: true }` for a document it could not find
`DATA` in. The first must move into the validation result strategy; the second must not.

### Findings about the skeleton

None for this group. All 116 rows' files, groups and input lists matched the handlers as read.
Two notes that are **not** disagreements:

- `ActivateObjectLow`'s file is `src/handlers/common/low/handleActivateObject.ts`, which the
  skeleton lists correctly — and the same handler function is what `ActivateObjects` (high)
  and `HandlerActivate` (compact) call. The skeleton's separate claim about five unregistered
  `common/low` `TOOL_DEFINITION`s is corrected in the **compact** group's Findings above.
- The fourteen `Activate<X>Low` rows and the fourteen `Activate<X>` high rows share one
  handler body each. Both sets are in this table because both are registered tool names, which
  is what the skeleton enumerates; each row's guarantees cell says so.

Check if transport request is required and valid.`, and otherwise parses `exc:exception/message` out of the body with a second `fast-xml-parser` pass. Then `return_error`. **Deletion masking applies**: ADT answers 200 with `isDeleted="false"` and a `del:message`, and this handler never reads the document. | Uppercases `ddl_name`.
